    2.8 CODE OF CONDUCT FOR MEDIA PRACTITIONERS
    Introduction                                                                                NOTES
         The Greek word "ethos," which means "way of living," is where the word
    "ethics" originates. It is a subfield of philosophy that investigates how people
    behave in society—specifically, how they behave as individuals—to distinguish
    between what is morally right and evil.
                                                                                                          TY
                                                                                     IV
         It explores what is morally just or unjust, right or wrong, good or evil, etc.
    Ethics looks at the rational justification for our moral judgements. The philosophy
                                                                                       ER
    focuses on the set of rules, regulations and principles by which people have to live.
    Every person has an opportunity to choose the right or wrong path in their life, but
    it must be based on the accepted standards, rules and regulations of society.

    Why do we need ethics?
                                                                                         SI
          1. Ethics helping us to direct the area between absolute rights and ethical
             wrong.                                                            N
          2. Ethics helps to make an unconditional right action work.
          3. Ethics helps to make ethical choices.
                                                                        U
          4. Ethics guide values within professions and groups. Y
                                                          R
          5. Ethics fits into the norms and standards of a group.
          6. Ethics develop skills in articulating our own values and principles.

                                              ER
          7. Ethics is a principal element of any happy, mature and healthy life.
          1. Accuracy and fairness: A journalist is responsible for making sure that
             information is distributed accurately and truthfully. No fact shall be one-
                                        H
             sided or the crucial facts intentionally misplaced by journalists and media
             houses. Groundless reports and assumptions should not be set forth as
             facts.            IC
          2. Act independently: Independence is a primary prerequisite of journalism,
                         D
             a cornerstone of democracy. Journalists must be independent voices; they
             should, with fair acts and criticism or report only as per facts. A journalist
                   N
             should not act with any particular interest of political parties, financial


E,
             institutions or any particular culture, business or corporates. Journalism
             has the capability to work as a supervisory body over those whose power


   PO
             and positions most affect citizens.
          3. Avoid fringe benefits: Investigative journalists should resist the
             temptations of fringe and unconventional benefits. A journalist should
             also avoid any quick gains and doubtful facts and, most importantly,
             verify authentic sources by the reporter at their own level.
D         4. Pre-publication verification: Journalist relies on a professional
D            discipline for verifying information. Journalists, when they are on
             reporting or giving any other information related to public interests,
             require utmost care by them. A journalist's transparent approach to Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                   Responsibility   49
    Media Ethics                                   evidence and factual accuracy from authentic sources alongside it needs
                                                   required verification at the time of reporting or writing an article in the
                                                   interest of the public.
                   NOTES
                                               5. Accountability: Accountability means taking responsibility for the work;
                                                  for example, when someone makes mistakes, they must fix them and  TY
                                                  regret must be sincere. Here, ethical accountability of journalists means
                                                  correcting errors, revealing media misconduct, welcoming criticism and
                                                  avoiding unethical practices.
                                                                                        IVER
                                               6. Respect facts: Journalists must respect the truth without any distortion
                                                  and manipulation of facts. The first responsibility of journalists is to


                                                                                            SI
                                                  uphold the truth for the facts and the public's right to the truth.

E,  Introduction
          The purpose of the media, including mainstream media, small and medium-

   PO
    sized media outlets, and community media, is to promote awareness of and
    participation in the right to free expression. State laws, norms, and procedures are
    used to impose media regulation, which differs around the world.
          They are there to safeguard freedom of expression, control media markets,
D   infrastructure, and freedom of the press, as well as to uphold technical standards

D   and, most importantly, safeguard public interests including media diversity,
    multiplicity, and variety.

   PO                                    2.14 PUBLIC INTEREST LITIGATION
                                         Introduction

D                                              Community Interest No law or conduct makes litigation clear. Judges are
                                         known to take the general public's intent into account. In Indian law, it refers to
D        Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                         legal action taken to defend the general welfare. It is legal action brought before a
                                         court of law by a third party other than the party who feels wronged, such as the

    58   Responsibility
    court itself. It is not required for the person who has had his or her rights violated to                     Media Ethics
    go before the court in person.
         The ability granted to the public by courts through judicial activism is known                 NOTES
    as public interest litigation. These situations could arise when the victim lacks the
    money to file a lawsuit or when his right to access the courts has been restricted or
    violated. Any public-spirited person may petition the court to take cognizance of
                                                                                                                  TY
    the subject and to begin proceedings.

    A. Who can file Public interest litigation?
         Any person or organisation may submit a PIL on their own behalf—that is, to
                                                                                       IVER
    protect or enforce a right granted to them by the government—or on behalf of a
    segment of society that is oppressed or poor and unable to uphold its own rights.

    B. Where Public Interest Litigation are filed?
                                                                                           SI
          Public Interest Litigation is an extension of writ jurisdiction. Therefore, under
    Article 32 of the Indian Constitution as well as Article 226 of the Indian   N
    Constitution, Public Interest Litigation may be brought before either the Honorable
                                                                           U
    Supreme Court or any High Court of India. To the Chief Justice of India or the
    Chief Justice of the High Court, however, even a brief letter or postcard may be
    sufficient.

   PO
    A. Introduction
         There are three main periods that occur on a timeline of media development:
    ancient media, old media, and modern media. It is important to consider the history
D   of media development that led us to new media in order to adequately define it.

D         Old Media emerged with the invention of the commercial printing press, early
    broadcasting, and video technologies, whereas Ancient Media is characterised by
    the rise of the written word. The development of digital technology and the internet, Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                           Responsibility   59
    Media Ethics                         which brought new tools and competences to the media opportunity, is responsible
                                         for the transition from Old Media to New Media.
                   NOTES                 B. Definition of new media

                                                                                                                  TY
                                               Robert Logan, in his book Understanding New Media, writes, "New media is
                                         very simply processed, stored, altered, retrieved, hyperlinked, and—perhaps most
                                         revolutionary of all—easily searched for and accessible," according to the


                                                                                        IV
                                         definition given above. Old media and new media can be distinguished by the fact
                                         that old media is, for the most part, mass media. In contrast to mainstream media,

                                                                                          ER
                                         each new media format is also quite interactive. Whether sending an email or using
                                         online collaboration tools, users of new media are active content and information
                                         creators.
                                                                                            SI
                                               New media theorist Lev Manovich describes new media that are native to
                                         computers or depend on them for distribution as websites, human-computer
                                         interaction, virtual worlds, virtual reality, multimedia, computer games, computer
                                                                                  N
                                         animation, digital video, special effects in movies, and interactive computer
                                         installations.
                                                                           U

                                         characteristics of new media. He considers the following as the main "principles" of

                                               ● Automation

                                  IC           Whatever issues and discrepancies result from the changes sparked by these

    2.17 SUMMARY
                                                                         N
                                                                   U
       ● Ethics: The Greek word "ethos," which meaning "way of living," is
         where the word "ethics" originates. It is a subfield of philosophy that
                                                           Y
         investigates how people behave in society—specifically, how they

                                                     R
         behave as individuals—to distinguish between what is morally right and
         evil.

                                         ER
       ● Need of ethics: Ethics helps us to direct the area between absolute rights
         and ethical wrong. Ethics helps to make an unconditional right action
         work. Ethics helps to make ethical choices. Ethics guide values within
                                   H
         professions and groups. Ethics fits into the norms and standards of a
         group. Ethics develop skills in articulating our own values and principles.
                           IC
         Ethics is a principal element of any happy, mature and healthy life.
       ● Theoretical philosophy: It investigates matters of knowledge, such as
                      D
         the existence of God and the universe's beginnings.
       ● Practical philosophy: It lessens the fact to be acted upon, for example,
               N
         axiology (study of the nature of value and valuation), semantics (the


E,
         meaning of phrases and words), ethics, etc.
       ● Loyalty: Here, an individual with an individual ethic of loyalty


   PO    establishes commitment and responsibility in all their relations and can be
         reliable by others to keep their trustworthy behaviour no matter the
         situation.
       ● Moral judgements: By projecting a term as moral judgement, we might

D        expect to meet measurements and definitions.
       ● Accuracy and fairness: A journalist is responsible for making sure that
D        information is distributed accurately and truthfully. No information shall
         be biased or the crucial facts intentionally misplaced by journalists and
                                                                                       Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                      Responsibility   63
    Media Ethics                               media houses. Groundless reports and assumptions should not be set forth
                                               as facts.
                   NOTES                    ● Group ethics: Group ethics can be stated in several ways. As the leader
                                              of the group, one must observe to code of ethics in demand to form the
                                                                                                                TY
                                              procedures and limitations of the group. As a member of a group, one
                                              should stand by ethical processes which ultimately reveal the
                                              fundamental values of the group.

                                              organisation. It is a method by which the press controls itself.

                                              manages and owns a transparent, wide-ranging and accurate television
                                              audience measurement system. It also offers an outfit of insights products
                                              planned for broadcasters, agencies and advertisers.
                                            ● Public Interest Litigation: In Indian law, it refers to legal action taken to
                                                                              N

                                              itself.                  U

    3.1 CONFLICT OF INTEREST
    A. Introduction                                                                                        NOTES
          Media ethics raises and manages ethical questions about how the media should
    act principally in a democratic society. Conflict of interest manages ethical obstacles
    for journalism that are currently present within the content creation procedures. In
    the days before internet technology, journalists, broadcasters, and publishers handled
                                                                                                                     TY
    all of the challenges. It was mainly made for the work of journalists.

    B. What is a conflict of interest?                                               IV
         Conflict of interest arises when an individual is dishonest due to a conflict ER
    between his/her professional obligations and personal interests. It happens when an
    organization or individual has some personal benefits like money, status and
    reputation that helps them to increase their reputation within the organization and
                                                                                         SI
    society. There are different ways in which disagreements between writers and
                                                                               N
    media and media buyers can arise. A few everyday situations of issues may one-
    sided include reporting about friends or family members. For example:
          1. A doctor should not operate on a family member.             U
          2. A lawyer who has protected a client in the past cannot be hired to take
             further legal measures for the same client.         Y
                                                           R
          3. A newsperson should not cover any news story in which she or he has a
             personal stake.

                                               ER
          4. Acceptance of benefits by journalists, for example, political parties and
             corporate organizations giving trips or gifts (Car, flat, property, etc.) to
             editors and journalists in exchange of supportive news and articles
             favouring them.            H
                                IC
         Generally, the purpose of the media is to provide accurate information to the
    public, as it really is. However, sometime unrealistic circumstances can influence a
    media person to get motivated to do something wrong.
                         D
    C. How to avoid conflict of interest?
                   N
         There are some ways to avoid conflict of interest:



E,        1. Don’t offer money to any demonstration groups, dispute groups and
             political parties.


   PO     2. Don’t accept any gifts or freebies from any corporates organisation,
             political parties and other wealthy groups.
          3. Don’t involve or take part in any political activity.
          4. Don't be too friendly and sociable with people around you.
D         5. Don’t put your personal opinion in sensational problems.

D         6. Don’t protect your family members or friends, if they have been doing
             any wrong.

    A. What is censorship?                     ER
         Censorship occurs when groups or individuals try to stop others from saying
    or depicting images, printing and words. It tries to find to limit the freedom of
                                        H
    thought and expression by limiting printed matter, spoken words, representative

                                IC
    communications, freedom of art, association, books, movies, music, television
    programmes, and internet sites.

                         D
          The networks of censorship can vary far and wide, from person-to-person
    communication and group-to-group communication. Similarly, a wide-ranging

                   N
    media, which includes literature, writings, electronic communication, music, art,
    and unexpected speech, can be put in danger of censorship. In each case, censorship


E,  is observed as falling offensive terminologies or expressions either directly or
    indirectly encouraging beneficial ones. Censorship mainly takes two fundamental


   PO
    practices: 1) State-forced and 2) second oneself (self-imposed).
          1. State-forced: The first practice is mandatory by a group in authority on
             the individuals, who are focused on the group’s authority and who are
             focused on the groups in influence on the individuals. It comprises
D            punishments, or their danger, that build a chilling outcome, provoking
             persons to force censorship on them to avoid punishment.
D         2. Second oneself or self-imposed: On the other side, the adverse support
             of evading punishments for an illegal appearance. Here, the second form Media Laws, Ethics and Social
             comprises a more constructive volunteer or controlled self-censorship by               Responsibility   71
    Media Laws                                     persons who select for themselves that controlling their forms of
                                                   communication has its own merit.

                                                                                        IV

    3.4 PERSONAL INTEGRITY                                                     N
    A. Introduction                                                      U
          If you have integrity, it means you are honest and firm in your moral
                                                                 Y
    principles. In today's journalism, there is much dubious news which is rumoured to
    be true. Due to easy access to social media, there are some individuals as well as
                                                           R
    groups of people who intentionally spread misinformation, rumours etc. This is one


                                               ER
    of the biggest reasons why the practice of journalistic integrity is essential.
         Over time, journalism ethics have changed. The majority of news organisations
    have a code of conduct. If professional journalists or news organisations violate these

                                        H
    moral guidelines, then their credibility will suffer.
          1. Strive for truth and report it: It is the first and foremost duty of the
                                IC
             journalist to report the truth to the audience. Journalists should be truthful
             and brave in reporting, gathering and taking information. According to

                         D
             the SPJ Code, journalists must be responsible for the veracity of their
             reporting, confirm material before disseminating it, and always rely on
                   N
             primary sources. Ethical journalism should always be precise and
             unbiased.


E,        2. Maximize harm: Ethical journalism treats co-workers, themes, sources,
             and members of the public as human beings worthy of respect. According

   PO        to the Code, a journalist must have empathy for anyone who might be
             harmed by news coverage. Journalists must also be watchful of cultural
             transformations when considering the potential reception of news or
             information.
D         3. Act Unconventionally: Helping the public is the primary duty of ethical
             reporting (not the command or the corporations). The "brown envelope"
D            trend in journalism is fundamentally at odds with the rule of journalism's
             independence and permits the media to deliver biased information to the Media Laws, Ethics and Social
             public.                                                                                Responsibility   73
    Media Laws                                 4. Be transparent or responsible: By taking responsibility for one's work
                                                  and being transparent about one's opinions, one practises ethical and
                                                  moral journalism. Journalists should describe moral selections and
                 NOTES
                                                  progressions to the viewers and should be aware of and openly admit any

                                                                                                                    TY
                                                  error. Additionally, errors should be swiftly and visibly corrected.
                                               5. Independence or impartiality: When reporting, journalists must be
                                                  independent or impartial, which means they must never include their

                                                                                        IV
                                                  opinions, feelings, or prejudices. Journalists should always speak


                                                                                          ER
                                                  neutrally and refrain from describing people or institutions in a
                                                  favourable or negative light. The writer can let each reader create their


                                                                                            SI
                                                  own perspective on the subject by only reporting the facts.

    3.8 PRINCIPLE OF HARM LIMITATION
                                                            R
    Introduction
                                                ER
          The damage principle holds that people's freedom of movement should only
    be limited to stop harm to other people. The harm principle, as articulated by John

                                         H
    Stuart Mill, is a theory which tries to, on the face of it, deliver an objective standard
    for acts that should be outlawed.

                                IC
         According to the harm principle, unevenly, the state may force an individual
    only if it can stop harm to others. Undoubtedly, this principle depends fatefully on

                          D
    what we recognize as harm and tiresome.

    What is the harm?
                    N
         To determine what constitutes injury is when the piercing point enters the


E,  picture. Although it might sound simple, it's actually not that simple. For example,
          1. Paternalism over children was satisfactory since children are not fully


   PO        skilled in responsibly exercising freedom, but paternalism over entirely
             self-directed adults is not.
          2. In case of certain kinds of speech can be as harmful sensitively as a
             physical attack. For example, individually insulting or establishing power
D            dominance and oppressing minorities.

D         3. According to Mill, you do not have the right to incite violence in
             response to damaging speech. Given that it causes pain and physical
             suffering, it is obviously destructive. However, he asserts that you do Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                      Responsibility   77
    Media Laws                                     have the freedom to damage another person's feelings because doing so
                                                   does not constitute harm.
                 NOTES                         An important aspect about the damage principle, which serves as the
                                         foundation for determining who is capable or deserving of enjoying their freedom,
                                                                                                                    TY
                                         is that it can be subject to personal, political, or cultural bias. We should always be
                                         wary of the potential biases that might influence who or what we apply rights and
                                         obligations to.

                                                                                        IV
                                                                         Check Your Progress

                                                                                          ER
                                            12. The harm principle, as articulated by ________, is a theory which tries to, on
                                                the face of it, deliver an objective standard for acts that should be outlawed.


                                         3.9 MEDIA BIAS AND SENSATIONALISM
                                         Biases within the media
                                                                                            SI
                                         Introduction                             N
                                                                            U
                                              Since 1947, discussions of media policy have frequently included references
                                         to media diversity. India's present media environment is ongoing, diverse, and
                                         regionalized. It has a weak regulatory environment and media owners who are
                                                                   Y
                                         dominant, powerful, and expanding. Investigating the media as a symbol of

                                                             R
                                         democracy is the idea.


                                                 ER
                                         Types of bias
                                               1. When stories are chosen or prejudiced to appease media owners who are
                                                  corporations, this is called corporate bias.

                                           H   2. When stories are chosen or prejudiced to suit advertisers, this is called
                                                  advertising bias.

                                  IC           3. Sensationalism bias favours the exceptional over the commonplace,
                                                  providing the appearance that uncommon occurrences like aeroplane

                           D                      crashes are more frequent than frequent occurrences like car accidents.
                                               4. The inclination of the mainstream media is to report what everyone else
                    N                             is reporting and to stay away from stories that might offend people.



E,
                                               5. Concision bias, the propensity to only report opinions that can be briefly
                                                  stated, weeds out more unique opinions that require more time to express.


   PO                                         Reporting that favours or disparages a certain race, religion, gender, age,
                                         sexual orientation, ethnic group, or even person is another kind of bias.

                                         Polarised Media
                                              Journalists are becoming more and more concerned that the Indian media has
D                                        polarised. Vinod Mehta, the editor-in-chief of the political magazine Outlook,
                                         penned the following: “You are either pro- or anti-Modi. No other space is
D        Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                         available.”

    involvement.          D

    behaviour and public opinion can ensure that the media behaves.


   PO    The main goal of ethical journalism is to serve the public by providing

    issues of public interest. Nowadays, ethical representation journalism is expected to
    cover news in trust with specific roles and responsibilities, or it should be
D   universally recognised.

                                         4.3 GENDER AND SEXUAL ORIENTATION
                                         Introduction
                                           H   Over the past few millennia, there have been numerous dramatic changes to

                                  IC     the position of women in India. The history of women in India has been turbulent,
                                         going from a largely unknown status in ancient times through the low moments of
                                         the medieval period to the support of equal rights by numerous reformers. There are
                           D             a few significant human issues in India that demand the government's full attention
                                         and care, and their successful resolution will play a critical role in the development
                    N                    of the country.


E,                                       Concept of feminism
                                              The term “feminism” creates different meanings for everybody. All


   PO                                    exchanged ideas about the word “equality”—“equality for women,” “equality for
                                         all genders,” “equality in the workplace,” “equality in other areas like political,
                                         social, economic, religious and others, etc.”


D
D        Media Laws, Ethics and Social
    90   Responsibility
                                                                                                      Marginalised Group Ethics



                                                                                                          NOTES


                                                                                                                    TY
                                                                                    IVER
                                     Fig. 4.1: Feminism

          Who is a feminist? Feminists are people who support feminism. What is
    feminism? Feminism is a theory that says that women and men should have
    equality which means that they have the same rights. Feminism is sometimes
                                                                                        SI
                                                                              N
    labelled as a philosophy in which women become leading over men. In fact, many
    feminists feel that the structure of male dominance, usually referred to as the
                                                                        U
    patriarchy, is harmful to men as well as women because of how it regulates
    “acceptable” masculine behaviour.

                                                                Y
         Feminism must operate within the very systems that women wish to change in
    order to thrive. Women must therefore reevaluate their relationship to power and
                                                          R
    power institutions. Power is complicated, but it's not destructive or hurtful, and it
    doesn't have to belong exclusively to patriarchy.

    Feminist activities in India              ER
         In India, feminist activism grew in popularity in the latter 1970s. The Mathura

                                        H
    rape case was one of the first national-level topics to unite the women's
    organisations. Widespread demonstrations broke out in 1979–1980 after policemen

                               IC
    accused of raping a little girl in a Mathura police station were found not guilty. The
    government was forced to change the Evidence Act, the Criminal Procedure Code,

                         D
    and the Indian Penal Code as well as add the category of Custodial Rape as a result
    of the protests, which were extensively covered in the national media. Since then,
                   N
    female activists have come together to advocate for causes like female infanticide,
    gender discrimination, women's health, and female literacy.


E,       New women-focused NGOs were able to get started in the 1990s thanks to
    donations from international donor organisations. Women's rights in India have

   PO
    been greatly aided by self-help organisations and NGOs like the Self Employed
    Women's Association (SEWA). Local movements now have a large number of
    female leaders.
         Statistics from 1992–1993 show that just 9.2% of Indian families were headed
D   by a woman. However, it was discovered that 35% or so of the households below

D   the poverty line were headed by women.

                                                                                             Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                            Responsibility   91
    Marginalised Group Ethics            Types of Feminism
                                             Following are some of the main subtypes of feminism:
                 NOTES                       1. Liberal feminism: One of the more prevalent forms of feminism is this.

                                                                                                                  TY
                                                Liberal feminists push for political and legal reform in order to achieve
                                                justice and sexual equality. This calls for equality in the workplace, in the
                                                classroom, and in political matters. It has a close relationship to the


                                                                                     IV

    106 Responsibility

          An act prohibiting the derogatory depiction of women in commercials, posters,
    publications, billboards, writings, paintings, photographs, and other media, as well
                                                                                                                TY
    as matters connected to or related to it. The Act imposes fines on those responsible

                                                                                    IV

    the portrayal of any ancient monuments bearing such images as well as the

                                                                                      ER
         The Indecent Representation of Women (Prohibition) Amendment Bill, 2012
          ● Smt. Krishna Tirath, Minister of State (Independent), Women and Child
            Development, submitted it before the Rajya Sabha on December 13, 2012.
                                                                                        SI
          ● The Act of 1986 makes it illegal to depict women indecently in
            publications, ads, artworks, writings, and other media.          N
                                                                       U
          ● With the passage of the Amendment, Bill 2012, the Act's application was
            expanded to include modern forms of communication such multimedia
            messages, internet-based Over-the-Top (OTT) services, and other
            programmes like WhatsApp, Skype, Snapchat, etc.    Y
                                                         R
          ● The Bill modifies the terms "distribution" and "advertisements" to
            encompass all forms of printed and electronic media, including new

                                              ER
            digital media, SMS, MMS or electronic form. It also changes the
            definition of ‘electronic form’; according to the Information Technology
            Act of 2000, it includes the creation and storage of such material on

                                        H
            magnetic, optical, and other media.
          ● The term "publish" will now include broadcasting, printing, or distributing

                               IC
            through audio-visual means as a result of the Bill's modifications.
          ● It permits any police officer with the level of Inspector or higher to look

                         D
            into crimes against this statute.
          ● Multiple offences' punishments are increased by the Bill in accordance
                   N
            with the Information and Technology Act, of 2000. For the first offence,
            the punishment was doubled to three years in prison and a fine between

E,          ` 50,000 and ` 1 lakh. Previously, the sentence was two years in prison
            and a fine of ` 2000. A third offence carries a sentence of between 2 and

   PO       7 years in prison and a fine of between 1 and 5 lakh rupees.

    19.2. IPC 292, 293
          Indian Penal Code (IPC) Sections 292 and 293 are the two sections of

D   vulgarism. It is a legal phrase that refers to anything morally repugnant and is
    frequently used interchangeably with the term pornography. The Indian Penal
D   Code's Sections 292 and 293 were enacted with the covert intention of preserving
    and defending the public right by outlawing the selling of obscene publications and
    literature, among other things.                                                     Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                         Responsibility   107
    Marginalised Group Ethics                Obscenity comprises pornography, which take account of sexual words,
                                        sexual act, etc. or it also comprises certain film, magazines, books, and other forms
                                        of entertainment are measured as obscene because they represent sex in very open
                NOTES
                                        way that some individuals find vulgar and bad-mannered.
                                        Section 292:
                                             Section 292 of the Indian Penal Code (IPC) says:
                                                                                                                   TY
                                                                                       IV
                                              Clause (1) to section 292 “A book, pamphlet, paper, writing, drawing,
                                        painting representation, figure or any other object, shall be deemed to be obscene if

                                                                                         ER
                                        it is lascivious or appeals to the prurient interest or if its effect such as tend to
                                        deprave and corrupt person, who is likely to read, see or hear the matter contained
                                        in such materials”.
                                                                                           SI
                                             Clause (2) to section 292 holds a person liable to punishment if he:
                                              ● Any obscene book, drawing, painting, paper, pamphlet, figure, or other

                                                                                 N
                                                object of any kind is sold, hired, publicly shown, distributed, or otherwise
                                                put into circulation. This also includes making, reducing, or having any
                                                                           U
                                                of these items in one's possession specifically for such reasons.
                                              ● Any offensive object for any of the aforementioned objectives through
                                                                  Y
                                                import, export, or conveyance.
                                              ● Participates in or profits from any activity in which he has knowledge or
                                                            R
                                                a good faith belief that any such offensive items were created for any of


                                                ER
                                                the aforementioned uses, produced, purchased, kept, imported, exported,
                                                transferred, shown in public, or otherwise placed into circulation.
                                              ● By publicly announcing or otherwise making known that a person is

                                          H     committing or is about to commit an act that is prohibited by this section.
                                              ● It offers to attempt to do any act which is an offence under this section.

                                 IC     Section 293:
                                        Section 293, Indian Penal Code, reads as below:
                          D             It punishes

                    N                         "Whoever sells, rents, distributes, exhibits, or circulates any such obscene
                                        object as is referenced in the preceding section, or offers or attempts to do so, shall


E,                                      be punished on first conviction with imprisonment of either description for a term
                                        that may extend to three years, and with fine that may extend to two thousand


   PO                                   rupees, and in the event of a second or subsequent conviction, with imprisonment
                                        of either description for a term that may extend to five years, and with fine that may
                                        extend.”

    4.9 SEXUAL HARASSMENT OF WOMEN AT
        WORKPLACE (PREVENTION, PROHIBITION AND
        REDRESSAL) ACT, 2013; SECTIONS 66A, 67 AND 67A                                                    NOTES
        OF THE INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY
        (AMENDMENT) ACT, 2008
    A. Sexual Harassment of Women at Workplace (Prevention,
                                                                                                                    TY
    Prohibition and Redressal) Act, 2013
    (a) Duty of the Employer or other responsible persons in workplaces and
    other institutions:
                                                                                    IVER
                                                                                        SI
         It is the responsibility of the employer or other responsible parties to take all
    necessary precautions to avoid or deter the commission of sexual harassment
    actions and to provide the mechanisms for their resolution, settlement, or
    prosecution.
    (b) Definition
                                                                              N
         For these purposes, sexual harassment comprises the following inappropriate
                                                                        U
    sexually motivated behaviour (whether expressed explicitly or implicitly):
         (i) Showing pornography;
         (ii) Sexually explicit comments;                       Y
        (iii) Physical progress and contact;              R
                                               ER
        (iv) Request or demand for sexual favours;
         (v) Any additional inappropriate sexual behaviour, whether it be physical,
             verbal, or nonverbal.
    (c) Preventive Steps:
                                        H
         All employers or persons in charge of the workplace, without prejudice to the
                               IC
    generality of this obligation, should take the following steps:
         (i) Sexual harassment should be prohibited, and suitable sanctions should be
                         D
             provided against offenders, in the norms and regulations of government

                     N
             and public sector organisations relating to behaviour and discipline.
         (ii) The explicit prohibition of sexual harassment as described above at work


E,            should be made known, publicised, and disseminated in the proper
              manners.


   PO   (iii) To further ensure that there is no hostile environment toward women at
              work and that no employee woman has reasonable grounds to believe she
              is disadvantaged in connection with her job, appropriate working
              conditions should be provided with regards to work, leisure, health, and

D             cleanliness.
        (iv) In order to comply with the Industrial Employment (Standing Orders) Act
D            of 1946, actions should be taken to include the aforementioned
             prohibitions in standing orders for private companies.
                                                                                             Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                            Responsibility   109
    Marginalised Group Ethics           (d) Criminal Proceedings
                                              When such behaviour constitutes a specific offence under the Indian Penal
                NOTES                   Code or another legal provision, the employer must take the required legal action
                                        by filing a complaint with the relevant authority.
                                                                                                                   TY
                                             In particular, it should make sure that any witnesses or victims of sexual
                                        harassment are treated fairly and without prejudice. It should be possible for sexual


                                                                                       IV
                                        harassment victims to request either the transfer of the offender or their own
                                        transfer.
                                        (e) Disciplinary Action
                                                                                         ER
                                              When such behaviour constitutes misbehaviour in the workplace as that term

                                                                                           SI
                                        is defined by the applicable service rules, the employer should take the appropriate
                                        disciplinary action in accordance with those rules.
                                        (f) Complaint Mechanism

                                                                                 N
                                             A suitable complaint system should be established in the employer's
                                        organisation for the redress of the victim's complaint, regardless of whether the
                                                                           U
                                        conduct in question amounts to a criminal offence or a violation of the service
                                        standards. Such complaint procedures should guarantee that complaints are handled
                                        promptly.
                                        (g) Complaints Committee
                                                                   Y
                                                            R
                                             A complaints committee, a particular counsellor, or other support services,


                                                ER
                                        including respecting confidentiality, should be available when needed through the
                                        complaint method mentioned in item 6 above.
                                              A woman should be in charge of the Complaints Committee, and at least half

                                          H
                                        of its members should be female. A third party, such as an NGO or another
                                        organisation with experience dealing with sexual harassment complaints, should be

                                 IC     included in the Complaints Committee in order to avoid the risk of any undue
                                        pressure or influence from senior levels.

                          D                   The Complaints Committee is required to provide an annual report to the
                                        relevant government agency detailing the complaints it has received and the actions
                    N                   it has taken. The employers and person in charge will also provide a report to the
                                        government department on the compliance with the aforementioned rules, as well


E,                                      as on the reports of the Complaints Committee.



   PO
                                        (h) Worker’s Initiative
                                              Sexual harassment concerns should be openly discussed in employer-
                                        employee meetings as well as in other suitable places where employees can voice
                                        their concerns.

D                                       (i) Awareness:
                                             Women's rights in this regard should be made more widely known by
D       Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                        appropriately notifying the rules (and any legislation, if it is passed on the matter).

    (j) Third-Party Harassment                                                                        Marginalised Group Ethics

         The employer and person in charge will take all necessary and reasonable
    actions to assist the affected individual in terms of assistance and preventive action                NOTES
    when sexual harassment arises as a result of an action or inaction by any third party
    or outsider.
         The Central/State Governments are urged to take appropriate action, such as
                                                                                                                    TY
                                                                                    IV
    passing legislation, to make sure that private sector employers follow the rules
    established by this directive. No rights guaranteed by the 1993 Protection of Human
    Rights Act would be adversely affected by these instructions.

    The Criminal Law (Amendment) Act of 2013                                          ER
    Introduction
         The Indian Criminal Law (Amendment) Act, 2013, was enacted by the Lok
    Sabha on March 19, 2013, and the Rajya Sabha on March 21, 2013. It modifies the
                                                                                        SI
                                                                              N
    1973 Code of Criminal Procedure, Indian Evidence Act, and Indian Penal Code to
    address sexual offences. The Bill obtained presidential assent on April 2, 2013, and
                                                                        U
    on April 3, 2013, it became law. As a result of the demonstrations around the 2012
    Delhi gang rape case, President of India Pranab Mukherjee first announced it as an
    Ordinance on April 3, 2013.
    1. Backdrop
                                                                Y
                                                          R
          The horrific Nirbhaya gang-rape case in Delhi, which occurred on December


                                              ER
    16, 2012, sparked widespread indignation and prompted the Indian government to
    put the problem of violence against women front and centre. Thus, a three-member
    judicial group headed by the former Chief Justice of India, J.S. Verma, was
    established; its main duties included reviewing potential criminal law revisions and
                                        H
    suggesting strategies for expedited trials and heavier punishments. The committee's
    recommendations were based on more than 80,000 proposals made by renowned
                               IC
    lawyers, social activists, and legal experts using a variety of techniques.
    2. The Criminal Law Amendment Act 2013 is also popularly known as the
                         D
       Anti-rape Act. It amends the following:

                   N
          ● The Indian Penal Code, 1806
          ● Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973


E,        ● The Indian Evidence Act, 1872



   PO
          ● Protection of Children from Sexual Offences Act, 2012
    3. What acts have been amended?:
         By this act, the following legislation has been amended:
          ● Indian Penal Code
D         ● Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973

D         ● Indian Evidence Act, 1872
          ● Protection of Children from Sexual Offences Act, 2012
                                                                                             Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                                                                                            Responsibility   111
    Marginalised Group Ethics           4. Throwing acid or attempting to throw acid
                                             The act of throwing or administering acid or an attempt to do so is now a
                NOTES                   crime under the revised Indian Penal Code. If someone throws acid and it results in
                                        whole or partial deformity or permanent harm, they will be sentenced to a
                                                                                                                    TY
                                        minimum of less than 10 years in prison, with the possibility of life. If someone
                                        makes an attempt to do the same, they will serve a minimum of 5 years in prison
                                        and a maximum of 7 years.
                                        5. Sexual Harassment
                                                                                       IVER
                                              A male engages in sexual harassment if he engages in any of the following:
                                        uninvited and explicit sexual advances, demands or requests for sexual favours, the


                                                                                           SI
                                        showing of pornography against the will of a woman, or making statements with a
                                        sexual connotation. Such guys would be sentenced to at least three years in prison,
                                        a fine, or both.
                                        6. Voyeurism
                                                                                 N
                                            Any guy who observes or records a woman doing a private act in settings

                                                                           U
                                        where she ordinarily expects to be undetected. Three years in prison is the bare
                                        minimum.
                                        7. Stalking
                                                                  Y
                                              Stalking is the practise of following or attempting to contact a woman for
                                                            R
                                        personal reasons despite the fact that she is uninterested; this includes doing so
                                        online, by email, or via any other similar medium. The maximum time behind bars

                                                  ER
                                        is three years. If a guy can show that the stalking was carried out to discover or
                                        prevent crime, or for any other acceptable motive, he can defend his innocence in
                                        such situations.

                                          H
                                        8. Rape


                                 IC          The law has set out different definitions of rape as well as different sanctions.
                                             The term rape has taken the place of sexual assault. The crime is now

                          D             exclusively committed against women by men; it is no longer a gender-neutral
                                        offence. The sentence that mentioned touching intimate areas has been deleted.

                                        (a) Information Technology Act, 2000


   PO                                         The Information Technology Act 2000 (ITA 2000) is commonly referred to as
                                        the IT Act. It is a law passed by the Indian Parliament on October 17, 2000 (Act No.
                                        21 of 2000). The primary statute in India addressing electronic trade and
                                        cybercrime is this one. A team of government representatives, led by Pramod

D                                       Mahajan, the previous minister of information technology, finalised the bill.
                                        President K.R. Narayana signed the legislation in May 2000 after it was approved

D       Media Laws, Ethics and Social
                                        at the 2000 budget session.

    Schedules make up the Act's 94 sections. The laws are applicable throughout India.           NOTES

          1. It makes it possible for the regulator to extend facilities to a subscriber in

    (b) Amendment, 2008                 H
                                IC
         The Amendment Act, also called as Information Technology Act, 2008 (IT
    Act 2008), is a significant addition to India’s ITA-2000. Although the Act was

                         D
    enacted by the Indian Parliament in October 2008, it wasn't until 2009 that it
    actually took effect. Recently, the Act has been managed and directed by the Indian

                   N
    Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT).
         Section 66A, which prohibited sending “offensive texts,” is where the

E,  Amendment is proposed. Additionally, it was added to Section 69, which granted
    authorities the authority to "intercept, monitor, or decode any information through

   PO
    any computer source." It also included new regulations covering voyeurism, child
    porn, pornography, and cyber terrorism.
         Without any debate, the amendment was adopted by the Lok Sabha on
    December 22, 2008. The Rajya Sabha gave it their blessing the following day.
D   Pratibha Patil, the president of India, signed it into law in February 2009.
